But vectorization across j is independent elements, good.\n\nBut a simpler high-performance approach: parallel over rows with blocking. Number of threads scaling fine for N>=512. Could also parallelize over i and maybe use collapse? Better parallelize i; each row independent, work evenly distributed. For N=512 and many cores, rows 512 enough. For large N fine. With -O3 (not fast-math), GCC can vectorize reductions? Actually GCC supports FP reduction vectorization only with -ffast-math/-fassociative-math. So no change.\n\nBut to be safest and good performance, I'll implement blocked j with k outer. Let me think which is faster. The j-block method: for each i (N iterations), traverse A row (N) and for each k, update a block of j columns of C (JB) with B row. Operations: same. Cache: C row block (JB doubles), B row (N doubles) streamed. A row fits. This is efficient, essentially like a \"rank-1 update\" per row, vectorizes over j nicely even without fast-math (independent mults/adds) -> vectorized, giving good throughput, likely better than strided dot. So this is great.\n\nHowever, for large N, B (N x N) is streamed from memory per i => N^3 memory traffic unless B fits in cache. Need i-blocking too to reuse B? Standard blocked matmul: block i and j so A block and B block stay in cache, k full. With j-block only, each i streams entire B (N^2). Total B traffic N^3. For N=512, B is 2MB, fits in L2/L3, so fine. For N large, worse. Could add i-blocking (IB rows) so B block reused across IB rows: for i-block, for j-block, for k, for i in block, for j in block. This changes nothing per element's k order. That gives better reuse of B: B tile N*JB loaded once per i-block. Standard.\n\nBut nested loops with i inner over block and j vectorized. This must be decided before the first\n// parallel region so that the OpenMP runtime sizes and places its team once.\nint chooseThreadCount(const size_t N) {\n    int numThreads = omp_get_max_threads();\n    const char* envThreads = std::getenv(\"OMP_NUM_THREADS\");\n    if ((envThreads == nullptr || *envThreads == '\\0') && N > 0) {\n        constexpr size_t rowsPerThread = 32;\n        const size_t tiles = ((N + IB - 1) / IB) * ((N + JB - 1) / JB);\n        size_t useful = (N + rowsPerThread - 1) / rowsPerThread;\n        useful = std::max<size_t>(useful, 1);\n        useful = std::min(useful, tiles);\n        numThreads = std::min(numThreads, static_cast<int>(useful));\n    }\n    return numThreads;\n}\n\nvoid initMatrix(std::vector<double>& mat, const size_t N) {\n    #pragma omp parallel for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0;
